\BookFrontChapter{Preface to Book v1.0.0}

Book v1.0.0 is the publication baseline for this guide and the canonical starting point for all future editions. It treats an agentic application as a governed distributed system: models may propose, while deterministic services own authority, state, effects, evidence, acceptance, and release.\par

The narrative follows one dependency chain. Foundations establish the deterministic shell; the reference architecture assigns ownership; the developer platform makes those controls reusable; patterns and anti-patterns guide topology; gateways and security enforce boundaries; observability and evaluation produce evidence; continuous learning converts evidence into governed change; and production operations keep the resulting system reliable and economical.\par

\section{Scope and claim discipline}

External facts, protocol behavior, framework capabilities, research findings, standards, and legal or regulatory context use numbered citations such as \BookCitation{R05}. Architecture choices without citations are recommendations authored for this book; they must be validated against the reader’s workload, jurisdiction, threat model, provider terms, and operating constraints. A source supports only the claim stated beside it—vendor documentation does not establish production fitness, community guidance is not law, and a benchmark result does not guarantee enterprise outcomes.\par

\section{Reproducible edition}

The accompanying Book v1.0.0 development package contains this canonical Markdown, the generated DOCX, source files, cover and diagrams, a reference registry, style tokens, templates, skills, build tools, validation tools, and release evidence. Future agents should modify the canonical Markdown and registries, never hand-format the release DOCX. The build and validation workflow is documented in the package README.\par

\BookFrontChapter{How to use this guide}

This guide is organized as one architecture story rather than a collection of independent topics. Read the executive map, then follow the lane that matches the decision. Every major chapter moves from problem and boundaries to architecture, implementation, failure modes, metrics, trade-offs, and review questions.\par

\section{Reference-guide contract}

\begin{enumerate}

\item Probabilistic components propose; deterministic components authorize and commit.

\item Every run is pinned to immutable prompt, Skill, workflow, policy, model-route, tool, evaluator, and knowledge versions.

\item Every side effect has one owner, an idempotency key, an effect record, and executable acceptance.

\item Every trust-boundary crossing revalidates identity, authority, content, and data policy.

\item Every material change is evaluated, approved at the right risk tier, canaried, observable, and reversible.

\item Every production failure can become an authorized regression case and improvement candidate.

\end{enumerate}

\BookPartPage{I}{FOUNDATIONS}{This Part defines the vocabulary and invariants used everywhere else. It begins with the progression from model call to production system, then turns model outputs into typed tool proposals, and finally places bounded reasoning behind a framework-neutral runtime contract. Read it before selecting orchestration patterns or products.}

\BookChapter{01}{From an LLM Call to a Production System}

\begin{bookcallout}[Northstar At This Point]
\textbf{NORTHSTAR AT THIS POINT}  Northstar Components wants to shorten supplier onboarding from twelve days to two. A prototype can summarize a supplier file, but production must also retrieve authorized records, request missing evidence, pause for people, screen sanctions, create auditable decisions, and survive days of inactivity.
\end{bookcallout}

\begin{bookcallout}[Design Decision]
\textbf{DESIGN DECISION}  Start Northstar as a governed distributed application with one bounded reasoning component, not as an autonomous chatbot.
\end{bookcallout}

\section{What this chapter delivers}

Explain why an agentic application is a distributed system and assign every authoritative decision to the correct component.\par

\section{The progression: model, tool, agent, system}

A language model maps input tokens to output tokens. A tool-using model can propose structured interaction with the environment. An agent chooses among steps using observations. An \textbf{enterprise agentic system} adds everything needed to make those choices safe and operational: durable state, identity, authorization, bounded resources, external-effect semantics, evidence, human control, deployment compatibility, evaluation, and incident recovery.\par

This distinction prevents a common category error. Better prompting can improve a reasoning step; it cannot provide transactional integrity, credential isolation, deterministic replay, tenant boundaries, or a provable audit trail. Those are properties of the surrounding system.\par

\BookTableStart
\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}Y{0.3133\linewidth}Y{0.3133\linewidth}Y{0.3133\linewidth}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Uncertainty} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Appropriate mechanism} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Authoritative owner} \\
\endfirsthead
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Uncertainty} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Appropriate mechanism} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Authoritative owner} \\
\endhead
Meaning of an ambiguous request & Model or classifier & Bounded agent step \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Which known path applies & Router plus deterministic validation & Route service / workflow \\
Unknown task decomposition & Planner plus plan compiler & Plan service / workflow \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Current business state & Database read through governed service & Domain system of record \\
Permission to act & RBAC/ABAC and delegated capability & Policy engine \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Whether a write committed & Effect receipt or reconciliation & Effect ledger + downstream system \\
Whether the run is complete & Executable acceptance criteria & Workflow / acceptance engine \\
\end{longtable}
\BookTableEnd

\subsection{The Northstar domain thread}

Throughout this book, Northstar builds a supplier-onboarding platform. The evolving system retrieves candidate records, validates documents, screens sanctions, requests corrections, coordinates specialists, waits for human decisions, creates supplier records, and preserves evidence. The use case is deliberately inconvenient: it crosses data boundaries, lasts days, includes ambiguous reasoning, performs protected writes, and requires audit. That makes it useful for testing whether an architecture is real.\par

\subsection{Anti-pattern — the autonomous monolith}

A single process receives a prompt, holds a broad user token, queries databases, calls arbitrary APIs, stores memory, retries writes, and declares completion. It is easy to demonstrate and nearly impossible to operate safely. A production decomposition does not remove intelligence; it gives intelligence explicit contracts and failure boundaries.\par

\section{The production problem}

An enterprise agent must translate an ambiguous goal into actions across systems that have strict identity, consistency, availability, privacy, cost, and compliance requirements. The LLM is nondeterministic; the environment is concurrent and partially failing; inputs may be adversarial; and many actions are irreversible. Production design therefore has to answer three questions for every step:\par

\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{May this principal perform this action on this resource now?}

\item \textbf{Can this step be retried, resumed, canceled, or compensated safely?}

\item \textbf{Can an operator later reconstruct what happened, why, under which versions and permissions?}

\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Core problem matrix}

\begin{longtable}{@{}Y{0.3133\linewidth}Y{0.3133\linewidth}Y{0.3133\linewidth}@{}}

\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Architectural problem} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Why ordinary prototypes fail} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Required production stance} \\

\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Architectural problem} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Why ordinary prototypes fail} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Required production stance} \\

Probabilistic control & A plausible model output is mistaken for a valid transition & Model proposes; deterministic code validates and commits \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}

Delegated authority & Shared service credentials turn an assistant into a privilege-escalation path & User + workload identity, token exchange, downscoping, audience binding \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Untrusted context & Retrieved instructions can redirect the agent or exfiltrate data & Provenance labels, content/instruction separation, egress controls, approvals \\

\rowcolor{BookLight}
Multi-agent coordination & Conversational delegation obscures ownership and creates loops & Typed task contract, single owner, bounded fan-out, budgets, terminal states \\
Memory and knowledge & Embeddings become an ungoverned shadow database & Separate stores, explicit write policy, ACL filtering, retention, provenance \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Model and vendor drift & Behavior changes without application code changing & Versioned model routes, eval gates, canaries, rollback, trace comparison \\
Operability & Logs show text but not causal execution & Distributed traces, structured events, replay, cost and policy telemetry \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Compliance & Decisions cannot be reproduced or explained & Immutable audit package, evidence chain, approvals, retention and legal holds \\
\end{longtable}

\section{Implementation reference}

\subsection{Provider-neutral agent-step contracts}

PYTHON · implementation excerpt\par

\begin{lstlisting}[style=bookcode,title={PYTHON},language=Python]
from __future__ import annotations
from datetime import datetime
from typing import Any, Literal
from pydantic import BaseModel, Field

class EvidenceRef(BaseModel):
    uri: str
    sha256: str
    source_revision: str
    authorization_decision_id: str

class ToolCall(BaseModel):
    tool_id: str
    arguments: dict[str, Any]
    logical_effect_key: str | None = None

class AgentDecision(BaseModel):
    disposition: Literal["answer", "needs_input", "invoke_tool", "delegate"]
    answer: str | None = None
    tool_call: ToolCall | None = None
    evidence: list[EvidenceRef] = Field(default_factory=list)
    uncertainties: list[str] = Field(default_factory=list)

class AgentStepRequest(BaseModel):
    run_id: str
    node_id: str
    state_version: int
    agent_version: str
    context_manifest_id: str
    eligible_tool_versions: tuple[str, ...]
    max_turns: int = Field(ge=1, le=20)
    deadline: datetime

class AgentStepResult(BaseModel):
    status: Literal["completed", "needs_input", "proposed_action", "failed"]
    decision: AgentDecision | None = None
    output_artifact_ids: tuple[str, ...] = ()
    usage: dict[str, int | float] = Field(default_factory=dict)
    error_code: str | None = None
\end{lstlisting}

\section{Debugging playbook}

\begin{enumerate}

\item Trace one request across model, workflow, tool, human, and record owners.

\item Find any place where prompt text is being treated as authoritative state.

\item Reproduce one partial failure and identify the missing durable fact.

\end{enumerate}

\section{Production checklist}

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} The model is a bounded reasoning component—not the owner of workflow state.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Identity, policy, state, effects, approvals, and acceptance each have an authoritative owner.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Autonomy is bounded independently across tools, data, time, cost, delegation, and publication.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Consequential effects are authorized, deduplicated, verified, and recoverable.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Operators can reconstruct the run from versions, evidence, decisions, and receipts.\par

\section{Exercises}

\begin{enumerate}

\item Draw the minimum production architecture for Northstar and label every authoritative store.

\item Convert a chatbot feature list into measurable outcome, authority, duration, data, and effect requirements.

\end{enumerate}

\rowcolor{BookLight}
Why should an agent be treated as a distributed system? & Model, workflow, queues, tools, humans, and external systems fail independently; duplicate delivery, waits, concurrency, and uncertain effects must be explicit. \\
How would you choose an autonomy level for a new use case? & Start with measurable value and risk; decompose autonomy; grant the minimum tools, data, time, spend, delegation, and effect authority needed. \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Which records must remain authoritative outside the model and prompt? & Run and workflow state, identity, policy decisions, budgets, approvals, business records, effect receipts, release versions, and audit evidence. \\

With the system boundary established, Chapter 2 converts model intent into typed, validated tool proposals and a deterministic commit path.\par

\BookChapter{02}{Tools, Structured Outputs, and the Deterministic Shell}

\begin{bookcallout}[Northstar At This Point]
\textbf{NORTHSTAR AT THIS POINT}  The prototype now proposes a call to create a supplier record. The JSON is valid and the rationale sounds convincing, yet the record may be stale, the user may lack write authority, and a retry could create a duplicate. Northstar introduces a deterministic shell around every model proposal.
\end{bookcallout}

\begin{bookcallout}[Design Decision]
\textbf{DESIGN DECISION}  Treat every model output as a typed proposal; validate, authorize, commit, verify, and accept outside the model.
\end{bookcallout}

\section{What this chapter delivers}

Turn model output into typed proposals and place validation, authorization, effects, and acceptance behind deterministic boundaries.\par

\section{Structured output is a protocol boundary}

Structured output is valuable because it makes a model decision parseable, versionable, and testable. It is not proof. A valid ToolIntent can still reference an ineligible tool, exceed delegated authority, target stale state, omit required evidence, or propose a duplicate mutation. The platform therefore follows \textbf{propose → validate → authorize → commit → verify → accept}.\par

A useful proposal is small. It names one disposition, one tool or delegation target, canonical arguments, evidence references, uncertainty, and the expected resource version. It does not carry credentials, policy truth, or an instruction to bypass a failing control.\par

\subsection{Deterministic acceptance}

Acceptance is not the model saying “done.” Northstar declares supplier onboarding complete only when required evidence artifacts exist, every screening result is fresh, approvals cover the exact proposal, all protected effects are committed with receipts, no unknown effect remains, and the supplier record satisfies domain invariants.\par

\subsection{Scaling the shell}

Scale tool work by workload and risk class, not with one global queue. Read-only retrieval can use high concurrency. Browser sessions, code sandboxes, high-risk writes, and human approvals need independent quotas and backpressure. The Tool Gateway is stateless where possible; manifests, policy, effect records, and receipts live in durable stores. Downstream concurrency limits, not model throughput, usually determine safe capacity.\par

\section{Design principles}

\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Deterministic shell, agentic core.} Put LLM reasoning inside typed activities; keep authorization, state transitions, budgets, validation, approvals, and side-effect commits in code.

\item \textbf{Least agency.} Grant the minimum autonomy, tools, data, time, tokens, fan-out, and mutation scope needed for the task.

\item \textbf{One run, one accountable owner.} A run may delegate, but exactly one coordinator owns completion, user communication, cancellation, and result aggregation.

\item \textbf{State outside the prompt.} Prompts are projections of durable state, not the database. Reconstruct context from authoritative stores on each step.

\item \textbf{Capability by contract.} Every agent and tool publishes a typed, versioned manifest; discovery never implies authorization.

\item \textbf{Policy at every boundary.} Evaluate authorization at ingress, retrieval, delegation, tool execution, data egress, memory write, and artifact publication.

\item \textbf{At-least-once by default.} Assume retries and duplicates. Require idempotency or compensation before a mutating capability can be certified.

\item \textbf{Evidence before autonomy.} Autonomy level rises only after offline evals, sandbox simulation, adversarial testing, canary evidence, and stable SLOs.

\item \textbf{Bounded recursion.} Cap steps, wall-clock time, spend, tokens, tool calls, delegation depth, parallel branches, and repeated failures.

\item \textbf{Open telemetry and portable state.} Use standard trace/event envelopes and keep the authoritative run record outside a proprietary agent runtime.

\end{enumerate}

\section{Where distributed systems must remain authoritative}

\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Traditional concept} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{What it should own} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Appropriate LLM role} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{If replaced by the LLM} \\

\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Traditional concept} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{What it should own} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Appropriate LLM role} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{If replaced by the LLM} \\
\endhead
State machine & Legal run states and transitions & Suggest next intent or plan node & Invalid/unreproducible transitions \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Durable workflow & Checkpoints, retries, waits, signals, child lifecycles, cancellation & Execute bounded reasoning activities & Lost work and duplicate effects \\
Queue & Load leveling, delivery, prioritization, consumer scaling & Estimate task type/priority within policy & Unbounded fan-out and overload \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Event sourcing/history & Ordered causal record and replay position & Summarize history for context & Narrative replaces evidence \\
Transaction/outbox/inbox & Atomic state/event publication and deduplication & Propose business change & Split-brain business state \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Idempotency/effect ledger & One logical effect and reconciliation & Generate or interpret user intent & Duplicate payments/messages/writes \\
Schema and type system & Interface validity and compatibility & Populate structured fields & Silent semantic drift \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Policy engine & Authorization, obligations, deny rules & Explain decision or request missing facts & Prompt-driven privilege escalation \\
Scheduler/timer & Precise durable wakeups and deadlines & Choose a policy-allowed schedule & Missed/duplicated jobs \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Lock/lease/concurrency control & Mutual exclusion where unavoidable; ownership leases & Choose work candidate & Concurrent corruption or deadlock \\
Circuit breaker/rate limiter & Protect dependencies and enforce fairness & Select evaluated fallback & Retry storms and quota collapse \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Database/search engine & Authoritative records and indexed retrieval & Natural-language query planning/synthesis & Hallucinated or stale state \\
Cryptography/KMS/secret broker & Keys, signatures, token issuance, rotation & None beyond requesting approved capability & Secret exposure and forged identity \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Tests/rules engine & Deterministic invariants and objective acceptance & Generate candidates and explanations & Self-certified incorrect output \\
\end{longtable}
\BookTableEnd

\begin{bookcallout}[Architecture note]
\textbf{BOUNDARY RULE: IF AN OPERATION MUST BE CORRECT EVERY TIME, SURVIVE A CRASH, ENFO}  Boundary rule: If an operation must be correct every time, survive a crash, enforce a permission, coordinate concurrent writers, or create an auditable effect, it belongs to deterministic infrastructure. The LLM may decide what to attempt; deterministic systems decide whether and how it is committed.
\end{bookcallout}

\section{Pattern reference — Tool Use}

\begin{bookcallout}[Architecture note]
\textbf{PROBLEM}  The model can identify an action or information need, but enterprise execution requires typed interfaces, current authorization, credentials, idempotency, and evidence.
\end{bookcallout}

\subsection{Architecture and flow}

The model proposes ToolIntent; the Tool Gateway validates the exact tool version and schema, reauthorizes the action and resource, mints a last-hop credential, invokes a connector, verifies the result, and records evidence.\par

\begin{enumerate}

\item Expose only tools eligible for the current run node.

\item Generate a minimal typed intent with evidence and uncertainty.

\item Validate schema, policy, risk, budget, approval, and resource version.

\item Invoke through a connector with timeout, idempotency, and egress controls.

\item Return a sanitized result or artifact reference and update durable state.

\end{enumerate}

\BookTableStart
\small

\endfirsthead
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Use when} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Do not use when} \\
\endhead
Use whenever the agent must read external truth or request an external effect. & Do not expose raw SDKs, database handles, credentials, or broad generic tools directly to model-generated code. \\
\end{longtable}

\subsection{Trade-offs}

\BookTableStart
\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}Y{0.4700\linewidth}Y{0.4700\linewidth}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Choice} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Consequence} \\
\endfirsthead
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Choice} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Consequence} \\
\endhead
Typed narrow tools & More interfaces to maintain; far stronger policy, testing, observability, and least privilege. \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Dynamic discovery & Smaller prompts and flexible catalogs; requires deterministic eligibility and version resolution. \\
\end{longtable}
\BookTableEnd

\subsection{Failures and controls}

\BookTableStart
\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}Y{0.4700\linewidth}Y{0.4700\linewidth}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Failure} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Control} \\
\endfirsthead
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Failure} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Control} \\
\endhead
Hallucinated tool or arguments & Schema and eligibility rejection. \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Duplicate mutation & Logical effect key, intent hash, downstream idempotency, and receipt. \\
Oversized or malicious output & Connector limits, content scanning, classification, and artifact indirection. \\
\end{longtable}
\BookTableEnd

\subsection{Minimum observability}

\begin{itemize}

\item tool and version

\item argument and result hashes

\item policy decision

\item credential lease ID

\item latency and retries

\item effect state and receipt

\end{itemize}

\section{Implementation reference}

\subsection{Governed Tool Gateway}

PYTHON · implementation excerpt\par

\begin{lstlisting}[style=bookcode,title={PYTHON},language=Python]
from __future__ import annotations
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from typing import Awaitable, Callable, Literal
from pydantic import BaseModel, Field
from .approvals import ActionProposal, ApprovalGrant, verify_approval
from .effects import EffectOutcomeUnknown, InMemoryEffectLedger

class ToolManifest(BaseModel):
    tool_id:str; version:str; description:str
    risk_class:int=Field(ge=0,le=4); mutating:bool; required_action:str
    timeout_seconds:float=Field(gt=0,le=300); approval_required:bool=False
    idempotency_required:bool=False

class InvocationContext(BaseModel):
    tenant_id:str; subject_id:str; actor_id:str; run_id:str; purpose:str
    allowed_actions:frozenset[str]; eligible_tool_versions:frozenset[str]
    policy_digest:str="policy:unspecified"; region:str="unspecified"

class ToolResult(BaseModel):
    status:Literal["succeeded","declined","unknown"]
    output:dict[str,object]=Field(default_factory=dict); effect_key:str|None=None

class ToolGateway:
    def __init__(self,effect_ledger:InMemoryEffectLedger|None=None)->None:
        self._tools:dict[str,RegisteredTool]={}; self._effects=effect_ledger or InMemoryEffectLedger()
    def register(self,manifest:ToolManifest,connector:Connector)->None:
        key=f"{manifest.tool_id}@{manifest.version}"
        if key in self._tools: raise ValueError(f"tool already registered: {key}")
        if manifest.mutating and not manifest.idempotency_required:
            raise ValueError("mutating tools must declare idempotency_required")
        self._tools[key]=RegisteredTool(manifest,connector)
    def discover(self,ctx:InvocationContext)->tuple[ToolManifest,...]:
        return tuple(item.manifest for key,item in sorted(self._tools.items()) if key in ctx.eligible_tool_versions and item.manifest.required_action in ctx.allowed_actions)
    async def invoke(self,*,ctx:InvocationContext,tool_version:str,arguments:dict[str,object],logical_effect_key:str|None=None,proposal:ActionProposal|None=None,approval:ApprovalGrant|None=None,actual_resource_version:int|None=None)->ToolResult:
        if tool_version not in ctx.eligible_tool_versions: raise PermissionError("tool is not eligible for this run node")
        item=self._tools.get(tool_version)
        if item is None: raise LookupError("eligible tool version is not registered")
        m=item.manifest
        if m.required_action not in ctx.allowed_actions: raise PermissionError("required action is outside delegated authority")
        if m.approval_required:
            if proposal is None or approval is None or actual_resource_version is None: raise PermissionError("proposal-bound approval is required")
            if proposal.model_dump(mode="json",exclude={"expires_at"}) != ActionProposal(tenant_id=ctx.tenant_id,run_id=ctx.run_id,actor_id=ctx.actor_id,tool_version=tool_version,logical_effect_key=logical_effect_key or "",arguments=arguments,expected_resource_version=proposal.expected_resource_version,policy_digest=ctx.policy_digest,expires_at=proposal.expires_at).model_dump(mode="json",exclude={"expires_at"}): raise PermissionError("proposal does not match invocation")
            verify_approval(proposal,approval,now=datetime.now(timezone.utc),actual_resource_version=actual_resource_version)
        if not m.mutating:
            return ToolResult(status="succeeded",output=await item.connector(arguments,None))
        if not logical_effect_key: raise ValueError("mutating tools require a stable logical effect key")
        intent={"tool":tool_version,"arguments":arguments,"run_id":ctx.run_id,"tenant_id":ctx.tenant_id}
        effect=self._effects.begin(logical_effect_key,intent)
        if effect.state=="committed": return ToolResult(status="succeeded",output=effect.receipt or {},effect_key=logical_effect_key)
        if effect.state in {"executing","unknown","intervention_required"}: raise EffectOutcomeUnknown(logical_effect_key)
        if effect.state=="declined": return ToolResult(status="declined",output=effect.receipt or {},effect_key=logical_effect_key)
        self._effects.mark_executing(logical_effect_key,f"req:{logical_effect_key}")
        try: output=await item.connector(arguments,logical_effect_key)
        except Exception as exc:
            self._effects.mark_unknown(logical_effect_key)
            raise EffectOutcomeUnknown(logical_effect_key) from exc
        committed=self._effects.mark_committed(logical_effect_key,output)
        return ToolResult(status="succeeded",output=committed.receipt or {},effect_key=logical_effect_key)
\end{lstlisting}

\section{Debugging playbook}

\begin{enumerate}

\item Compare the raw model proposal with the validated command and list rejected fields.

\item Inspect the policy decision and resource version immediately before commit.

\item Replay the same logical mutation and verify one receipt.

\end{enumerate}

\section{Production checklist}

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Every model output is classified as a proposal by default.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} State transitions, policy, budgets, business rules, and protected commits sit behind deterministic interfaces.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Proposals are minimal, typed, evidence-linked, and explicit about uncertainty.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Resource versions and obligations are rechecked immediately before commit.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Completion uses deterministic acceptance criteria.\par

\section{Exercises}

\begin{enumerate}

\item Define a typed proposal and deterministic acceptance function for supplier screening.

\item Write five invariants that must be true before supplier activation can commit.

\end{enumerate}

\section{Technical review questions}

\BookTableStart
\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}Y{0.4700\linewidth}Y{0.4700\linewidth}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Question} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Strong answer} \\
\endfirsthead
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Question} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Strong answer} \\
\endhead
What belongs in the agentic core versus the deterministic shell? & Semantic interpretation, evidence synthesis, and bounded planning in the core; authority, state, policy, concurrency, effects, and acceptance in the shell. \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Explain propose–validate–commit. & The model emits a typed proposal; deterministic services validate schema, authority, risk, budget, evidence, and version; only a domain service commits and records a receipt. \\
Why is structured model output necessary but insufficient? & It reduces parsing ambiguity but does not prove truth, authorization, freshness, business validity, idempotency, or safety. \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Where should completion be decided? & In an acceptance engine or workflow guard using required artifacts, effect states, policy obligations, and domain invariants—not from a model’s “done” message. \\
What is the trade-off of a stronger deterministic shell? & More contracts and platform complexity, but improved safety, replayability, testing, provider portability, incident recovery, and auditability. \\
\end{longtable}
\BookTableEnd

\section{Transition to Chapter 3}

With tools bounded, Chapter 3 defines the runtime contract that keeps applications portable across agent frameworks and model providers.\par

\end{bookquote}

\begin{bookquote}

This chapter uses the OpenAI Agents SDK as a concrete adapter. Enterprise applications should compile platform-owned contracts into this or another framework through conformance-tested adapters; Chapters 4–7 define the portable architecture.

\end{bookquote}

\begin{bookcallout}[Northstar At This Point]
\textbf{NORTHSTAR AT THIS POINT}  Northstar adopts OpenAI Agents SDK for its compact agent loop, tools, handoffs, structured outputs, tracing, sessions, and human-interruption surfaces. The architecture team refuses to let SDK state become workflow truth or to expose raw enterprise credentials to the agent process.
\end{bookcallout}

\begin{bookcallout}[Design Decision]
\textbf{DESIGN DECISION}  Place the OpenAI Agents SDK behind AgentRuntime; pin 0.22.2 and keep workflow, authority, credentials, effects, and audit platform-owned.
\end{bookcallout}

\section{What this chapter delivers}

Use OpenAI Agents SDK 0.22.2 as a bounded, replaceable runtime adapter rather than the enterprise control plane.\par

\section{Framework baseline and placement}

This edition pins openai-agents==0.22.2 and openai>=2.25.0 as an implementation baseline. The SDK provides useful application-level primitives: Agent, Runner, function tools, agents-as-tools, handoffs, guardrails, structured output, sessions, tracing, MCP integration, deferred tool loading, ToolSearchTool, tool namespaces, human interruption, serializable RunState, and sandbox-oriented agent surfaces.\par

Northstar uses those primitives for \textbf{one bounded agent activity}. A durable workflow calls a platform-owned AgentRuntime; the OpenAI adapter builds the eligible agent and tool set, invokes Runner, and translates the result back into provider-neutral contracts. This makes the SDK valuable without making it the owner of long-lived business truth.\par

\BookTableStart
\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}Y{0.4700\linewidth}Y{0.4700\linewidth}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{SDK concern} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Northstar placement} \\
\endfirsthead
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{SDK concern} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Northstar placement} \\
\endhead
Agent instructions and structured output & SDK adapter, pinned in release manifest \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Turn loop and local handoff & SDK worker, bounded by external limits \\
Sessions / RunState & Optional resumable runtime state; never canonical workflow state \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Function tools and MCP & Narrow façades that call the Tool Gateway \\
Guardrails & Defense in depth; policy still enforced outside the model \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Tracing & Exported and correlated with workflow/effect/audit IDs \\
Approval interruption & Mapped to durable ApprovalRequest and workflow wait \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Credentials and business writes & Tool Gateway and domain services only \\
\end{longtable}
\BookTableEnd

\subsection{Upgrade discipline}

An SDK upgrade is a behavior change even when public types remain compatible. Northstar runs adapter contract tests, recorded trajectory replay, tool-schema tests, approval interruption tests, model-route evaluations, load tests, and canary releases. Active workflows retain the SDK and agent-definition versions in their RunManifest.\par

\section{Architectural role}

The OpenAI Agents SDK is best treated as a \textbf{bounded agent harness}, not the platform kernel. It contributes model-facing ergonomics—agent definitions, tools, structured outputs, local handoffs, run context, streaming, guardrails, and tracing hooks. Enterprise durability, delegated authorization, business transactions, approvals, effect reconciliation, tenant isolation, and audit remain platform responsibilities.\par

\section{Stable runtime contract}

Keep public Run APIs and workflow history independent of SDK types. A platform-owned boundary can be expressed as:\par

TEXT · implementation excerpt\par

\begin{lstlisting}[style=bookcode,title={TEXT}]
AgentRuntime.execute(AgentStepRequest) -> AgentStepResult
\end{lstlisting}

AgentStepRequest pins run and node identity, state version, agent and model route, authorized context manifest, eligible tools, capability ceiling, output schema, resource limits, deadline, and trace context. AgentStepResult returns a typed disposition, immutable artifact references, proposed actions, a concise evidence-grounded summary, usage, trace references, and a structured error.\par

\section{One bounded step}

\begin{enumerate}

\item The durable workflow schedules a reasoning activity with pinned versions and a deadline.

\item Context is materialized from an authorized ContextManifest.

\item The adapter creates SDK agents and only the tool façades eligible for this node.

\item External controls enforce turns, time, tokens, cost, tool calls, parallelism, and delegation depth.

\item The SDK executes the local model/tool loop.

\item The adapter validates the structured result and records usage and trace linkage.

\item Workflow code—not the SDK—chooses the next durable transition.

\end{enumerate}

\section{Bounded Python adapter}

PYTHON · implementation excerpt\par

\begin{lstlisting}[style=bookcode,title={PYTHON},language=Python]
import os
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Literal, Protocol

from agents import Agent, RunConfig, RunContextWrapper, Runner, ToolExecutionConfig
from agents.decorators import tool
from pydantic import BaseModel


class ToolGateway(Protocol):
    async def invoke(self, *, tenant_id: str, subject_id: str,
                     actor_id: str, run_id: str, tool: str,
                     arguments: dict[str, object]) -> dict[str, object]: ...


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PlatformContext:
    tenant_id: str
    subject_id: str
    actor_id: str
    run_id: str
    gateway: ToolGateway


class StepDisposition(BaseModel):
    status: Literal["completed", "needs_input", "proposed_action"]
    summary: str
    evidence_artifact_ids: list[str]


@tool
async def retrieve_supplier_policy(
    ctx: RunContextWrapper[PlatformContext], country: str
) -> dict[str, object]:
    """Retrieve authorized policy through the enterprise Tool Gateway."""
    return await ctx.context.gateway.invoke(
        tenant_id=ctx.context.tenant_id,
        subject_id=ctx.context.subject_id,
        actor_id=ctx.context.actor_id,
        run_id=ctx.context.run_id,
        tool="supplier.policy.read.v2",
        arguments={"country": country},
    )


agent = Agent[PlatformContext](
    name="Supplier analyst",
    model=os.environ["OPENAI_AGENT_MODEL"],
    instructions=(
        "Analyze only supplied authorized evidence. Return a structured "
        "disposition. Never claim that an external write occurred."
    ),
    tools=[retrieve_supplier_policy],
    output_type=StepDisposition,
)


async def execute_step(request, context: PlatformContext) -> dict[str, object]:
    result = await Runner.run(
        starting_agent=agent,
        input=request.input_items,
        context=context,
        max_turns=request.max_turns,
        run_config=RunConfig(
            workflow_name="supplier-onboarding",
            group_id=request.run_id,
            trace_include_sensitive_data=False,
            trace_metadata={
                "run_id": request.run_id,
                "agent_version": request.agent_version,
            },
            tool_execution=ToolExecutionConfig(
                max_function_tool_concurrency=2,
            ),
        ),
    )
    output = StepDisposition.model_validate(result.final_output)
    return {
        "status": output.status,
        "decision_summary": output.summary,
        "output_artifacts": output.evidence_artifact_ids,
        "usage": str(result.context_wrapper.usage),
    }
\end{lstlisting}

The model and SDK never receive a reusable production credential. The tool façade submits identifiers and typed arguments to the gateway, which reauthorizes the exact resource, mints a short-lived last-hop credential, enforces effects and quotas, and sanitizes the result.\par

\section{Handoffs and specialists}

Use Agent.as\_tool() when a manager must retain control of the final result. Use an SDK handoff when conversational control genuinely transfers inside one bounded activity. Use a durable TaskEnvelope and child workflow when the work needs independent ownership, retries, waits, deadlines, scaling, cancellation, or a cross-service trust boundary. A conversational handoff must never silently transfer budget, authority, task ownership, or cancellation responsibility.\par

\section{Guardrails, state, and retries}

SDK guardrails are valuable for early input, tool, and output checks. They do not replace canonical schema validation, a policy decision point, Tool Gateway authorization, or domain transaction rules. SDK sessions can assist local continuity, but run state, plan revisions, budgets, approvals, effects, policy decisions, and compliance evidence remain authoritative elsewhere.\par

Assign one retry owner per failure class: the workflow owns activity retries; the Model Gateway may perform short transport retries inside one recorded attempt; the Tool Gateway follows the tool manifest and effect protocol; an unknown mutation is reconciled before any retry.\par

\section{Adoption test}

Adopt the SDK only after proving that tools cannot bypass gateways, every invocation is externally bounded, handoffs map to durable ownership correctly, sensitive tracing is disabled or isolated, protected commits require deterministic validation, and another runtime could replace the adapter without migrating public APIs, workflow history, effects, artifacts, approvals, or audit.\par

\section{Grounding, corrections, and evidence boundary}

\BookTableStart
\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}Y{0.3133\linewidth}Y{0.3133\linewidth}Y{0.3133\linewidth}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Claim or correction} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Grounded statement} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Source IDs} \\
\endfirsthead
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Claim or correction} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Grounded statement} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Source IDs} \\
\endhead
Pinned SDK behavior & OpenAI Agents SDK 0.22.2 was released 9 September 2026. The project uses 0.Y.Z versioning and remains fast-moving. Deferred tool search is Responses-only, needs openai>=2.25.0, and requires exactly one ToolSearchTool when deferred function surfaces are used. & OAI-RELEASE; OAI-TOOLS \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Pause/resume boundary & RunState is the SDK pause/resume object. Custom context serialization is conservative; rebuild live gateways and secrets rather than serializing them blindly. Independently restored states must not resume concurrently against one session history. & OAI-RUNSTATE \\
Scope of SDK approval & An SDK approval interruption pauses a call; it does not by itself prove enterprise authorization, separation of duties, resource freshness, or idempotent effect semantics. The gateway must verify those controls. & OAI-HITL \\
\end{longtable}
\BookTableEnd

Evidence anchors: OAI-RELEASE; OAI-TOOLS; OAI-RUNSTATE; OAI-HITL. Full URLs, authority, version, and scope appear in Appendix M.\par

\section{Implementation reference}

\subsection{OpenAI Agents SDK enterprise adapter}

PYTHON · implementation excerpt\par

\begin{lstlisting}[style=bookcode,title={PYTHON},language=Python]
"""OpenAI Agents SDK 0.22.2 adapter.

Requires openai-agents==0.22.2 and openai>=2.25.0. Framework-owned approval
interrupts complement—but do not replace—the enterprise proposal and effect ledgers.
"""
from __future__ import annotations
import os
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Annotated, Any, Literal, Protocol
from agents import Agent, RunConfig, RunContextWrapper, Runner, ToolSearchTool, tool_namespace
from agents.decorators import tool
from pydantic import BaseModel, Field

class EnterpriseToolGateway(Protocol):
    async def invoke(self,*,tenant_id:str,subject_id:str,actor_id:str,run_id:str,tool_version:str,arguments:dict[str,object],logical_effect_key:str|None=None,approval_id:str|None=None,proposal_hash:str|None=None)->dict[str,object]: ...

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PlatformContext:
    tenant_id:str; subject_id:str; actor_id:str; run_id:str; purpose:str; gateway:EnterpriseToolGateway

class SupplierDecision(BaseModel):
    disposition:Literal["request_evidence","ready_for_approval","reject"]
    rationale:str=Field(min_length=1,max_length=1_000)
    evidence_artifact_ids:list[str]

@tool(defer_loading=True)
async def get_supplier_profile(ctx:RunContextWrapper[PlatformContext],supplier_id:Annotated[str,"Canonical supplier candidate ID"])->dict[str,object]:
    """Load the authorized profile for one canonical supplier candidate."""
    return await ctx.context.gateway.invoke(tenant_id=ctx.context.tenant_id,subject_id=ctx.context.subject_id,actor_id=ctx.context.actor_id,run_id=ctx.context.run_id,tool_version="supplier.profile.read@2.1.0",arguments={"supplier_id":supplier_id})

@tool(defer_loading=True)
async def screen_supplier(ctx:RunContextWrapper[PlatformContext],supplier_id:Annotated[str,"Canonical supplier candidate ID"])->dict[str,object]:
    """Run the governed screening capability for one supplier candidate."""
    return await ctx.context.gateway.invoke(tenant_id=ctx.context.tenant_id,subject_id=ctx.context.subject_id,actor_id=ctx.context.actor_id,run_id=ctx.context.run_id,tool_version="supplier.screen@3.0.0",arguments={"supplier_id":supplier_id})

@tool(needs_approval=True)
async def create_supplier_record(ctx:RunContextWrapper[PlatformContext],supplier_id:str,expected_version:int,approval_id:str,proposal_hash:str)->dict[str,object]:
    """Commit only after SDK interruption and gateway verification of the exact proposal."""
    return await ctx.context.gateway.invoke(tenant_id=ctx.context.tenant_id,subject_id=ctx.context.subject_id,actor_id=ctx.context.actor_id,run_id=ctx.context.run_id,tool_version="supplier.create@1.4.0",arguments={"supplier_id":supplier_id,"expected_version":expected_version},logical_effect_key=f"{ctx.context.run_id}:supplier.create:{supplier_id}",approval_id=approval_id,proposal_hash=proposal_hash)

supplier_tools=tool_namespace(name="supplier_onboarding",description="Read-only supplier profile and screening capabilities.",tools=[get_supplier_profile,screen_supplier])

def build_supplier_agent()->Agent[PlatformContext]:
    model=os.environ["OPENAI_AGENT_MODEL"]
    return Agent[PlatformContext](name="Supplier onboarding analyst",model=model,instructions="Use only supplied, authorized evidence. Load the supplier_onboarding namespace when evidence is required. Protected writes require the direct create_supplier_record tool and must preserve approval IDs and proposal hashes. Never claim an external write without its receipt.",tools=[*supplier_tools,create_supplier_record,ToolSearchTool()],output_type=SupplierDecision)

async def execute_bounded_run(input_items:str|list[dict[str,object]],context:PlatformContext):
    """Return RunResult so the platform—not an exception string—owns pause/resume."""
    return await Runner.run(starting_agent=build_supplier_agent(),input=input_items,context=context,max_turns=6,run_config=RunConfig(workflow_name="northstar-supplier-onboarding",group_id=context.run_id,trace_include_sensitive_data=False,trace_metadata={"run_id":context.run_id,"purpose":context.purpose}))

def serialize_interrupted_run(result:Any)->str:
    if not result.interruptions: raise ValueError("run has no pending approval")
    # Custom PlatformContext contains a live gateway; configure an application serializer
    # or persist identifiers separately and rebuild context when restoring the snapshot.
    return result.to_state().to_string()

async def approve_in_memory_and_resume(result:Any,interruption_index:int=0):
    """Minimal SDK flow; production first verifies the enterprise ApprovalGrant."""
    state=result.to_state(); state.approve(result.interruptions[interruption_index])
    return await Runner.run(build_supplier_agent(),state)
\end{lstlisting}

\section{Debugging playbook}

\begin{enumerate}

\item Capture the platform request and SDK adapter request and diff their responsibilities.

\item Verify which SDK state is persisted and whether its version is pinned.

\item Disable one tool at the gateway and confirm the agent cannot recover ambient access.

\end{enumerate}

\section{Production checklist}

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} The SDK sits behind a platform-owned AgentRuntime adapter.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Only governed tool façades enter the agent process.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Turns, time, tokens, cost, tool calls, and delegation have external limits.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Local handoffs are distinct from durable task ownership.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Retry, state, trace privacy, and release compatibility are coordinated outside the SDK.\par

\section{Exercises}

\begin{enumerate}

\item Implement a minimal AgentRuntime protocol and an OpenAI Agents SDK adapter.

\item Design a compatibility test that proves an SDK upgrade preserves platform contracts.

\end{enumerate}

\section{Technical review questions}

\BookTableStart
\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}Y{0.4700\linewidth}Y{0.4700\linewidth}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Question} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Strong answer} \\
\endfirsthead
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Question} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Strong answer} \\
\endhead
Where should the OpenAI Agents SDK sit in an enterprise architecture? & Inside a bounded worker behind AgentRuntime, called by a durable workflow and connected to model and tool gateways through platform contracts. \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
What should the SDK not own? & Authoritative workflow state, delegated authorization, credentials, business transactions, effect reconciliation, enterprise approvals, and audit evidence. \\
When should you use an agent-as-tool, a handoff, or a durable child task? & Use an agent-as-tool when a manager retains control; a handoff when conversational control transfers; a durable task when work needs separate ownership, waits, retries, scaling, or recovery. \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
How do you make SDK tools safe? & Supply a minimal set, use typed façades, reauthorize exact resources at the Tool Gateway, mint last-hop credentials, enforce idempotency, and sanitize results. \\
How do you keep the SDK replaceable? & Store platform contracts, workflow history, artifacts, effects, approvals, and public APIs in provider-neutral forms; confine SDK types to the adapter. \\
\end{longtable}
\BookTableEnd

\begin{bookcallout}[Architecture note]
\textbf{PART II}  \textbf{AGENTIC DESIGN PATTERNS}  Choose and implement the smallest pattern that resolves the real uncertainty: ReAct, routing, planning, reflection, human control, and durable execution.
\end{bookcallout}

\section{Transition to Chapter 4}

The runtime now has a stable boundary; Chapter 4 places it inside the complete enterprise reference architecture.\par

\BookPartPage{II}{ENTERPRISE REFERENCE ARCHITECTURE}{This Part converts the foundational invariants into an enterprise system of record. It assigns ownership across experience, application, runtime, knowledge, gateway, security, governance, and operations planes, then specifies the APIs and records that let those planes evolve independently.}

\BookChapter{04}{Enterprise Agentic AI Reference Architecture}

\section{Purpose and principles}

The enterprise architecture places probabilistic reasoning inside explicit experience, runtime, workflow, knowledge, integration, security, evaluation, and operations planes. The agent is a decision component—not the workflow, authorization service, database, policy engine, or proof of completion.\par

\BookDiagram{assets/diagrams/reference-architecture.png}{Figure 4-1 — Enterprise agentic AI layered reference architecture}{reference-architecture}

\BookTableStart

\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Plane} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Responsibilities} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Authoritative records} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Boundary} \\
\endfirsthead
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Plane} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Responsibilities} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Authoritative records} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Boundary} \\
\endhead
Experience and channels & Intent, interaction, review, accessibility, recourse & Request and interaction record & UI state is not approval or workflow truth \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Agent applications & Domain instructions, topology, response assembly & AgentSpec and application release & Cannot mint authority or directly commit protected effects \\
Runtime and workflow & Run graph, deadlines, budgets, retries, cancellation, checkpoints, joins & Run and TaskEnvelope state & Transcript is evidence, not execution state \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Context, RAG, memory, Skills & Authorized evidence, provenance, snapshots, governed persistence & ContextManifest, MemoryRecord, Skill manifest & Retrieved text remains untrusted data \\
Gateways and integration & LLM routing, tool façades, MCP/A2A, APIs, events & RoutingDecision, ToolCall, protocol envelope & Revalidate identity, policy, schema, and tenancy \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Security and governance & Identity, least privilege, RBAC/ABAC, guardrails, approval, DLP, audit & PolicyDecision, CapabilityEnvelope, ApprovalGrant & Protected actions fail closed; ambiguous effects become unknown \\
Evaluation and operations & Tracing, evaluation, SLOs, incidents, release, capacity, learning & Trace graph, EvaluationResult, ChangeBundle, ReleaseManifest & Monitoring cannot self-promote behavior change \\
\end{longtable}
\BookTableEnd

\section{End-to-end responsibility flow}

\BookDiagram{assets/diagrams/request-sequence.png}{Figure 4-2 — Request-to-verified-outcome sequence}{request-sequence}

\begin{enumerate}

\item Authenticate the initiating subject and workload; establish tenant, purpose, risk, deadline, and trace.

\item Admit a run and pin all behavior, policy, data, evaluator, and dependency versions.

\item Build an authorized ContextManifest; preserve provenance and untrusted-content labels.

\item Let the model propose a typed decision. Policy determines eligible models, tools, data, and actions.

\item Bind protected changes to an ActionProposal and exact, expiring ApprovalGrant.

\item Commit through a tool façade with short-lived credentials, idempotency, EffectRecord, and reconciliation.

\item Determine accepted, blocked, needs-input, failed, or unknown-effect using executable business criteria.

\item Join traces, evaluations, human feedback, cost, latency, incidents, and outcomes for controlled improvement.

\end{enumerate}

\rowcolor{BookLight}

\rowcolor{BookLight}

\section{Architecture decisions}

Centralize identity, policy, gateways, tool/effect contracts, telemetry schemas, evaluation infrastructure, release evidence, and incident controls. Federate domain prompts, accepted outcomes, tools, Skills, knowledge, datasets, and SLOs to accountable owners. Standardize interfaces and evidence—not every framework implementation.\par

\section{From boxes to contracts}

A reference architecture becomes implementable when every arrow has an API or event contract and every record has one authoritative writer. Northstar separates a global definition and governance plane from regional execution cells. Cells contain run control, durable workflow, bounded agent workers, model and Tool Gateways, context services, effect ledger, artifact store, and regional telemetry/audit pipelines.\par

The public Run API is asynchronous and idempotent. Commands carry expected state version and deadline; accepted work returns 202 plus an operation or run resource. Status and events are durable and paginated. Large inputs and outputs use immutable artifact references rather than oversized JSON. Public contracts do not expose SDK messages or provider-specific run objects.\par

\subsection{Core authoritative records}

RunManifest, WorkflowExecution, TaskEnvelope, AgentStep, ContextManifest, CapabilityEnvelope, PolicyDecision, CredentialLease, ToolManifest, ApprovalRequest, EffectRecord, Artifact, EvaluationReport, and ReleaseManifest each have one owner, version, retention policy, and consistency boundary.\par

Scale stateless APIs horizontally, partition state by tenant and run, shard workflow history and trace indexes, and isolate workload classes through queues and concurrency pools. Cell placement enforces region and residency while bounding blast radius.\par

\section{Core platform APIs and contracts}

\subsection{Run API}

\BookTableStart
\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}Y{0.4700\linewidth}Y{0.4700\linewidth}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Operation} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Semantics} \\
\endfirsthead
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Operation} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Semantics} \\
\endhead
POST /v1/runs & Create idempotently from agent/workflow reference, goal, inputs, subject delegation, autonomy request, budgets, and callback preferences \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
GET /v1/runs/\{id\} & Return authorized status, current stage, owner, progress, budgets, waits, and result/artifact references \\
GET /v1/runs/\{id\}/events & Cursor-based event history or server-sent stream; redaction and history limits by role \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
POST /v1/runs/\{id\}:signal & Send typed, authenticated input such as clarification, approval, callback, or document-ready event \\
POST /v1/runs/\{id\}:cancel & Request hierarchical cancellation with reason and compensation policy \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
POST /v1/runs/\{id\}:update & Optimistic, authorized patch to budget, priority, assignee, or plan; may require approval \\
POST /v1/runs/\{id\}:fork & Create a sandbox replay/fork from checkpoint with pinned or explicitly changed versions; never reapply live effects \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
GET /v1/runs/\{id\}/audit & Return evidence manifest to authorized compliance/incident roles \\
\end{longtable}
\BookTableEnd

\subsection{Artifact contract}

Every artifact has artifact\_id, immutable version, schema/media type, content hash, creator run/node/agent, source references and hashes, tenant, owner, classification, residency, ACL/purpose policy, confidence/quality metadata, retention/deletion linkage, and optional signature. Large artifacts move by reference; callers receive short-lived authorized URLs rather than embedded payloads.\par

\subsection{Effect receipt}

A mutating tool returns a structured receipt: logical effect ID, idempotency key, downstream request ID, target resource and before/after version, committed/declined/unknown state, timestamp, actor/subject, compensation reference, verification query, and audit link. A timeout without a receipt is unknown, not failed.\par

\section{Storage architecture}

\BookTableStart
\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}Y{0.3133\linewidth}Y{0.3133\linewidth}Y{0.3133\linewidth}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Store} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Recommended contents} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Consistency and controls} \\
\endfirsthead
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Store} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Recommended contents} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Consistency and controls} \\
\endhead
Workflow history store & Events, timers, activity results, signal cursor & Engine-managed durability; replay semantics; encrypted per environment/tenant \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Relational operational DB & Run projections, manifests, budgets, ownership, approvals, effect ledger, dedup keys & Strong transactions, optimistic versions, row-level security \\
Object store & Prompts by digest, large tool outputs, artifacts, evidence bundles, replay fixtures & Immutable/versioned objects, hashes, retention lock, tenant keys \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Search/vector index & ACL-aware chunks and embeddings derived from source systems & Source revision/provenance, pre-retrieval authorization, rebuild/delete pipeline \\
Event log/broker & CloudEvents, status changes, business triggers & At-least-once, partitions, schema registry, outbox/inbox, DLQ/quarantine \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Trace/metric store & OTel spans, metrics, logs with content references & Tiered retention, sampling, redaction, restricted content access \\
Audit/WORM archive & Policy decisions, approvals, effects, release evidence & Append-only, signed/hashed, legal hold and regulatory retention \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Secret broker / KMS & Connector secrets, token-exchange keys, encryption keys & No secrets in prompts/state; short-lived delivery; rotation and hardware-backed keys \\
Cache & Model, retrieval, tool-read, schema and policy-safe results & Scope-aware keys, TTL/freshness, encryption, no cross-tenant leakage \\
\end{longtable}
\BookTableEnd

A vector database is an index, not the source of truth; an agent transcript is not workflow state; a trace store is not the audit ledger; and a queue is not a business transaction. Keeping these responsibilities separate prevents attractive shortcuts from becoming systemic risks.\par

\section{Implementation reference}

\subsection{Core platform data contracts}

PYTHON · implementation excerpt\par

\begin{lstlisting}[style=bookcode,title={PYTHON},language=Python]
from __future__ import annotations

from datetime import datetime
from typing import Any, Literal

from pydantic import BaseModel, Field


class EvidenceRef(BaseModel):
    uri: str
    sha256: str
    source_revision: str
    authorization_decision_id: str


class ToolCall(BaseModel):
    tool_id: str
    arguments: dict[str, Any]
    logical_effect_key: str | None = None


class AgentDecision(BaseModel):
    disposition: Literal["answer", "needs_input", "invoke_tool", "delegate"]
    answer: str | None = None
    tool_call: ToolCall | None = None
    evidence: list[EvidenceRef] = Field(default_factory=list)
    uncertainties: list[str] = Field(default_factory=list)


class AgentStepRequest(BaseModel):
    run_id: str
    node_id: str
    state_version: int
    agent_version: str
    context_manifest_id: str
    eligible_tool_versions: tuple[str, ...]
    max_turns: int = Field(ge=1, le=20)
    deadline: datetime


class AgentStepResult(BaseModel):
    status: Literal["completed", "needs_input", "proposed_action", "failed"]
    decision: AgentDecision | None = None
    output_artifact_ids: tuple[str, ...] = ()
    usage: dict[str, int | float] = Field(default_factory=dict)
    error_code: str | None = None


class SkillRequirement(BaseModel):
    capability: str
    purpose: str
    maximum_risk: int = Field(ge=0, le=4)
    required_data_classes: frozenset[str] = frozenset()
    region: str


class PlanNode(BaseModel):
    id: str
    capability: str
    depends_on: tuple[str, ...] = ()
    maximum_cost_usd: float = Field(ge=0)
    maximum_attempts: int = Field(ge=1, le=10)
    risk_class: int = Field(ge=0, le=4)


class CandidatePlan(BaseModel):
    revision: int = Field(ge=1)
    nodes: tuple[PlanNode, ...] = Field(min_length=1, max_length=100)
\end{lstlisting}

\subsection{CloudEvents and outbox contracts}

PYTHON · implementation excerpt\par

\begin{lstlisting}[style=bookcode,title={PYTHON},language=Python]
from __future__ import annotations

from datetime import datetime
from typing import Any, Literal

from pydantic import BaseModel, Field, field_validator


class CloudEvent(BaseModel):
    """CloudEvents 1.0-compatible application envelope."""

    specversion: Literal["1.0"] = "1.0"
    id: str = Field(min_length=1)
    source: str = Field(min_length=1)
    type: str = Field(min_length=1)
    subject: str | None = None
    time: datetime
    datacontenttype: str = "application/json"
    dataschema: str | None = None
    data: dict[str, Any]
    tenant_id: str
    traceparent: str | None = None

    @field_validator("source")
    @classmethod
    def source_is_uri_reference(cls, value: str) -> str:
        if ":" not in value and not value.startswith("/"):
            raise ValueError("source must be a URI-reference")
        return value


class OutboxRecord(BaseModel):
    event: CloudEvent
    aggregate_id: str
    aggregate_version: int = Field(ge=0)
    state: Literal["pending", "published", "dead_letter"] = "pending"
    publish_attempts: int = Field(default=0, ge=0)


def deduplication_key(event: CloudEvent) -> tuple[str, str]:
    """CloudEvents producers make (source, id) unique for each distinct event."""

    return event.source, event.id
\end{lstlisting}

\section{Debugging playbook}

\begin{enumerate}

\item Trace one API command through stores, queues, services, and contracts.

\item Find any record with two authoritative writers.

\item Map a cell failure to its intended containment boundary.

\end{enumerate}

\section{Production checklist}

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Every record and transition has one authoritative owner.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Public contracts remain independent of model providers and SDK types.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Every trust boundary states authentication, authorization, validation, and data handling.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Regional execution can continue safely on pinned releases during control-plane disruption.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Cells, queues, and local dependencies bound failure domains.\par

\section{Exercises}

\begin{enumerate}

\item Define Run, Task, Tool, Approval, Effect, Artifact, and Release contracts.

\item Select a store and consistency model for each authoritative record.

\end{enumerate}

\section{Technical review questions}

\BookTableStart
\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}Y{0.4700\linewidth}Y{0.4700\linewidth}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Question} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Strong answer} \\
\endfirsthead
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Question} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Strong answer} \\
\endhead
Walk through the major planes of an enterprise agent platform. & Experience and edge, global definition, run control, durable execution, agent and model, capability, data and context, trust, and operations—with explicit ownership and interfaces. \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Why separate the control plane from the execution plane? & They have different scaling, availability, data-residency, and failure characteristics; immutable definitions can fan out while active tenant runs stay regional. \\
Where are the most important trust boundaries? & User-to-edge, knowledge-to-model, model-to-workflow, workflow-to-tool, sandbox-to-network, peer-to-platform, and platform-to-model provider. \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Why use cell-based deployment? & Additive scale, tenant placement, residency, bounded blast radius, independent canaries, and predictable stateful-service limits. \\
How should execution behave during a control-plane outage? & Continue compatible in-flight work on pinned last-known-good bundles, apply local denies and kill switches, and restrict high-risk admission when freshness cannot be established. \\
\end{longtable}
\BookTableEnd

\end{bookquote}

\section{Platform promise}

The developer platform is a reusable paved road that lets teams build differentiated agents without repeatedly rebuilding identity, policy, tool safety, tracing, evaluation, durable execution, and delivery infrastructure. It is not a mandatory monolith: applications use platform-owned contracts and replaceable adapters.\par

\BookDiagram{assets/diagrams/developer-platform.png}{Figure 6-1 — Agentic developer platform and SDK capability stack}{developer-platform}

\section{Portable contracts and multi-framework support}

\BookDiagram{assets/diagrams/portability.png}{Figure 6-2 — Platform contracts with replaceable framework and provider adapters}{portability}

Use AgentSpec, RunContext, TaskEnvelope, ToolSpec/ToolCall, ContextManifest, MemoryRecord, EvaluationResult, and ReleaseManifest as stable contracts. Compile them into framework/provider adapters. Standardized contracts, MCP, APIs, events, agent-to-agent protocols, gateways, and shared platform services provide interoperability without leaking vendor-native message objects into applications. Compatibility suites verify cancellation, tools, streaming, approvals, errors, telemetry, usage, and version behavior.\par

\section{Developer lifecycle}

\BookDiagram{assets/diagrams/developer-lifecycle.png}{Figure 6-3 — Local-to-production agent lifecycle}{developer-lifecycle}

Create from an approved template; attach tools, Skills, context, and memory; configure model policy, permissions, budgets, and review; test deterministic logic, contracts, simulations, adversarial cases, and failures; evaluate quality, safety, latency, and cost; package immutable artifacts with locks, SBOM, signature, provenance, and ReleaseManifest; promote through policy gates; observe correlated production traces; improve through controlled ChangeBundles.\par

\section{SDK example}

PYTHON · implementation excerpt\par

\begin{lstlisting}[style=bookcode,title={PYTHON},language=Python]
agent = platform.agent(
    spec="supplier_onboarding@3.2.0",
    model_policy="quality-balanced@7",
    permissions=["supplier:read", "screening:propose"],
    budgets={"tokens": 18_000, "usd": 0.45, "deadline_s": 90},
)
agent.attach_skill("supplier-screening@2.4.1")
agent.attach_tool(screen_supplier, risk="protected", effect="screening.create")
agent.use_context(source="supplier-policy", snapshot="approved-latest")
agent.use_memory(policy="tenant-user-preferences@3")
report = platform.test(agent).simulate("supplier-onboarding-regression")
platform.release(agent, require=["quality", "security", "cost", "rollback"])
\end{lstlisting}

The fluent API is convenience. The compiled contracts, policy decisions, tool/effect evidence, traces, and release manifest are the stable interface.\par

With platform responsibilities separated from application logic, Chapter 7 specifies the developer workflow that makes the paved road usable.\par

\BookChapter{07}{Developer Experience, Testing, and Delivery Lifecycle}

\section{Golden path}

Developer experience should compress setup and diagnosis time while making authority, versions, risk, evidence, and cost visible. A convenient SDK that hides those concerns transfers complexity to production incidents.\par

\section{Testing and simulation pyramid}

\BookDiagram{assets/diagrams/test-pyramid.png}{Figure 7-2 — Testing and simulation pyramid}{test-pyramid}

Deterministic units verify schemas, policy helpers, acceptance, routes, reducers, calculations, and state transitions. Contract tests verify identity, idempotency, timeout/unknown-effect semantics, protocol capability, errors, and telemetry. Component evaluations measure retrieval, tools, arguments, structured output, safety, and memory. Trajectory simulation tests planning, handoffs, loops, deadlines, cancellation, duplicates, dependency degradation, and human delay. End-to-end staging uses exact model, identity, gateway, data, and integration versions. Canary and shadow evaluation use explicit stop and rollback conditions.\par

\section{CLI and delivery workflow}

BASH · implementation excerpt\par

\begin{lstlisting}[style=bookcode,title={BASH},language=bookbash]
agentctl init supplier-onboarding --template regulated-workflow --risk high
agentctl dev --scenario late-sanctions-response --trace
agentctl test --unit --contracts --failure-injection
agentctl eval --dataset supplier-regression@12 --compare production
agentctl package --lock --sbom --sign
agentctl promote --from staging --to production --canary 5%
agentctl traces diff run:failed run:accepted
agentctl rollback supplier-onboarding@3.2.0 --to 3.1.4
\end{lstlisting}

Use one immutable artifact across environments; bind endpoints, identities, secrets, sources, quotas, regions, and review queues at deployment. Pin SDKs, adapters, model aliases, tool schemas, Skills, MCP servers, policy bundles, evaluators, and knowledge snapshots. A model/provider swap is a behavior change—not harmless configuration.\par

\section{Troubleshooting}

Start from accepted business outcome and walk backward through the causal run graph. Find the first divergent span: input classification, source/freshness, prompt, route, model, tool, arguments, policy, effect, approval, or acceptance. Do not “fix the prompt” until evidence locates the failing responsibility.\par

\section{Transition to Chapter 8}

The delivery path is established; Chapter 8 packages repeatable behavior as versioned Skills with explicit contracts and lifecycle controls.\par

\BookChapter{08}{Skills as Versioned Capabilities}

\begin{bookcallout}[Northstar At This Point]
\textbf{NORTHSTAR AT THIS POINT}  Teams begin copying prompts and tool wrappers between agents. Results diverge, fixes do not propagate, and no owner can prove which behavior reached production. Northstar introduces a Skill as the unit of reusable capability.
\end{bookcallout}

\begin{bookcallout}[Design Decision]
\textbf{DESIGN DECISION}  Make the Skill manifest the versioned capability contract and resolve exact releases through policy and evaluation evidence.
\end{bookcallout}

\section{What this chapter delivers}

Define Skills as governed packages of instructions, tools, knowledge, policy, workflows, tests, and operations metadata.\par

\section{Solution blueprint}

\BookFigureCaption{Figure 10. Skills as Versioned Capabilities.}

\endfirsthead
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{State owner} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Trust boundary} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Scale unit} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Failure containment} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Core contracts} \\

Skill Registry and resolver & semantic discovery to policy-bound resolution & skill family · registry partition & one skill version & SkillManifest · SkillRequirement · SkillRelease \\

\section{Skill anatomy}

A Skill is the smallest independently owned and versioned package that lets an agent perform a meaningful capability. Its manifest names interface schemas, instructions, eligible tools, knowledge sources, optional workflow template, policy constraints, side-effect class, observability fields, evaluation suite, owner, lifecycle, and immutable digest.\par

Skills separate reuse from copy-and-paste. An application requests a capability such as supplier.evidence.read; it does not embed a specific prompt and connector. Discovery searches a catalog. Resolution then filters by tenant, purpose, subject and actor grants, data class, region, risk, compatibility, release stage, health, and cost before pinning one exact version.\par

\subsection{Skill lifecycle}

Draft → Certified → Staged → Active → Deprecated → Revoked is evidence-driven. Certification includes contract, unit, simulation, trajectory, threat, failure-injection, load, and operator-readiness checks. Deprecation retains a compatibility window for active runs. Revocation removes the Skill from new resolution immediately and may trigger workflow pause or migration by risk class.\par

\subsection{Skill contract surface}

\begin{itemize}

\item describe(requirement) -> candidates

\item resolve(requirement, run\_envelope) -> SkillRelease

\item invoke(step\_request) -> AgentStepResult

\item evaluate(release, dataset\_version) -> EvaluationReport

\item promote(release, stage, evidence) -> ReleaseDecision

\end{itemize}

The registry scales as a read-heavy control-plane service with immutable manifests, version indexes, cached eligibility metadata, and regional last-known-good replicas. Runtime execution remains in regional cells.\par
